There was a man in the land (chora) of Ausitis, whose name was Job. And this man was just, blameless, and religious (theosebēs), having abstained from all evil. He had seven sons and three daughters, and his livestock were: seven thousand sheep, and three thousand camels, and five hundred pairs of oxen, and five hundred grazing donkeys; and he had a numerous staff (hypēresia) with great works on land; and that man was rich among

the Orientals. And his sons would go out every day (?), having a feast with one another (in the house of their eldest brother), taking with them also their three sisters who ate and drank with them. And when the days of feasting were passed, Job was accustomed to send (to his livestock) ... to take a calf and offer it for their souls (psychē); for Job said: "Perhaps (mēpōs) my sons have thought evil in their hearts toward God?" This is what Job did every day.

alone escaped: I have come to inform you." While this one was still speaking, another messenger came; he said to Job: "A fire came from heaven, consumed the sheep and devoured the shepherds as well; and I alone escaped: I have come to inform you." While this one was still speaking, another messenger came, and he said to Job: "The horsemen formed three platoons against us, surrounded the camels and captured them; and they killed the servants with the sword; and I alone escaped: I have come to inform you." While this one was still speaking, another messenger came; he said to Job: "While your sons and your daughters were eating and drinking in the house of their eldest brother, all of a sudden a violent wind arrived from the desert and struck the four corners of the house; the house fell upon your children; they perished; and I alone escaped: I have come to inform you."

And the Lord said to the devil: "Behold, I deliver him into your hands; only spare his life." And the devil went out from the presence of the Lord, and he struck Job with a malignant sore from his feet to his head. time (χρόνος) having passed, his wife said to him: "How long will you be patient saying: I will remain yet a little while, waiting in the hope (ἐλπίς) of my salvation? For behold, your memorial has disappeared from off the earth, your sons and your daughters, the pains and the sufferings of my womb with which I have been painfully afflicted for nothing. And you are sitting under infectious worms, watching in the heat and the dew; as for me, I am a wanderer and a slave, (circling) from place to place, from house to house, waiting for the moment when the sun will set to rest from my sufferings and my afflictions which now envelop me; but say a word to the Lord and die." When his three friends had heard of all the evils that had befallen him, they came to him each from his own country (χώρα): Eliphaz, king of the Temanites; Bildad, tyrant (τύραννος) of the Shuhites, and Zophar, king of the Minnaeans; they came to him together to console him and visit him. Having seen him from afar, they did not recognize him; and having let out a great cry, they wept; each of them having torn his garment (στολή), they cast earth upon their heads. They sat near him seven days and seven nights; none of them spoke, for they saw that the wound was extremely painful and grave.

Eliphaz the Temanite answered and said: "Has one spoken to you many times in a hurtful way in affliction? But who could endure the violence of your words? While you have instructed many people and strengthened the hands of the weak, restored the sick by your words, firmed up feeble knees, now that suffering has come upon you and touched you, you have become agitated; is your fear not unreasonable? And is your hope in the malice (κακία) of your ways? Reflect: who has perished being pure, or when have the just perished radically? As we have seen those who cultivate disorder (ἄτοπα), those who sow it will reap

not a form (μορφὴ) before my eyes: but it was a breath and a voice that I heard: What then? Will a man be pure before the Lord! Or will a man be blameless by his works? If he does not trust in his servants, and if in his angels he notices something that suffers, as for those who dwell in houses of mud, and we ourselves are of the same mud, he sends them a plague like the moth, and from morning until evening, they are no more; because they cannot help (βοηθεῖν) one another, they have perished; he has blown upon them, they have withered and they have perished, because they had no wisdom.

For me, I have seen the foolish take root, and suddenly their fortune has been swallowed up; their sons will be far from well-being; they will be tormented at the gates of their inferiors, and there will be no one who saves them; what those have amassed, the just will consume, and they will not be pulled from misery; their strength will be exhausted; for it is not from the earth that pain will come out, and it is not upon the mountains that suffering will grow; but it is man who is born in pain, as the little eagles fly in the heights. are under the sky, who raises the humble and resurrects those who are lost, who overturns the plans of the crafty (πανοῦργος) and of those whose hands do nothing just, who takes the wise in their science and has put in suspense the plan of the devious. Darkness will come upon them in the day; they will grope at noon as in the night; they will perish in war (πόλεμος); the weak will escape from the hand of the one who had power; hope will come to the weak; the mouth of the violent will be closed. Happy is the man whom the Lord will correct! Do not receive with reluctance the lesson of the Almighty. It is he who gives suffering, and again he who gives rest; if he gives the blow, his hands then heal; six times he will save you in necessity: the seventh time evil will no longer reach you; in famine, he will save you from

death; in war, he will save you from iron. He will put you under cover from the scourge (μάστιξ) of the tongue and you will not fear when evils come. You will mock the violent and the libertines (ἄνομος) ... the wild (ἄγριος) beasts (θηρίον) will be in peace (εἰρήνη) with you. Then you will know that your house will be in peace and that the management of your tent (σκηνή) will not sin in any way; you will know that your seed is abundant; your sons will be like the vegetation of the countryside; you will go to the grave like wheat harvested at its time, or like the heap of grain on the threshing floor, which one brings in at its moment. Behold the things as we have meditated upon them, and these are the things that we have learned. It is for you to know in yourself what you have done."

Job answered and said: "If someone weighed my grievance and put together

ππδκδο ᾿ ἄδητ εν οὐλλδν Εὐοοτι. ΚΕ ἼῊ οι δ- άατς ΕΟ ἐϑοσε ποθ ἧστε τον, δνδ ΟΞ ΕΚ Ἄγε πδοδι δου, τυροσπεα τὰρ ἅτε πιός ΟΕΧΥ Ῥεν πδολὰ ΜῊ ἐπε πουλλλον Ὁ οὐκ χαπτδόπος, εν διωδπερϑπο πολι σου ΟΕΘΟΥ ΣΈ χλλλον. ΟὟ τδρ ἈΗ ἀπδα ἐὸν ἘπλΎΡ ὅθε οὐδ ἡ τλοῦυ τελην τακζ το πεαφεῦ τὰ ΔῈ ἀΛΔῚῚ ἡπεΟΛΗ σε οὐδὸν ϑίσεν πεσουονθα ρὲ ουόντεα Ὦρεῦ Χαλλδ. ΔΗ ΟΕ ΔΟΎΕΧ αὰκ ἩΔΊΘΛΛΟ τ ΟὙῸΝ ΘΆΜΩΕΙ
in a balance my afflictions, truly they would be heavier than the sand of the sea; but to what point would my words be bad? For the arrows of the Lord are in my body (σώμα) and their heat drinks my blood; if I begin to speak, they prick me. What then? Will the wild ass cry for nothing? Will he cry unless he is seeking his food? Or will the ox bellow over his manger, when he has food? Will one eat bread without salt? Is there flavor in hollow words? It is not possible that my soul (ψυχή) should stop; for I see my food putrid like the smell of the lion. If he would give me, if what I ask (αἰτεῖν) would happen and if the Lord would give me my hope! May the Lord begin and may he touch me; may he not make me die absolutely! May my tomb be my city on the walls of which I used to leap! I will not use

ΔῊ ΟὙπολλ πατν πε Ἰδόξολ τὲ Ἰδοδλρέ ουϑολ πε... ἡ Ἴονθτδ ΔῈ ΔΟΟΎΕΙ ἄλλλον. οὐ δάχᾶτ πἤοσας κε ᾧρτδι. στρ ἘΧΆΎΤΟΤΗΣ Ὧδε ἧπε πὸ δόδπστϑο ἄχλλον, ΧΑτΟ ΣΟΎ ἘφΟΣ Ὲ τ Ἐπθεν ερὸν ΡΤ ποτ λλο πούαρελλ ἐδ λλο Ὅς ἈΦΡΗΤ πουολ Ἐδύυσενι. restraint; for I have not lied to the words of my God which are sacred. What then is my strength that I should be patient? What is my duration (χρόνος) that my soul should accept? Is my strength the strength of stone? Is my flesh of bronze? ... Help is far from me, and pity has abandoned me; the visitation of the Lord has disdained me. My relatives have not looked at me, like a torrent that has been lost, or like a flood, after having passed me by; those who feared me have now risen against me; like snow (χιῶν) or ice (κρύσταλλος) that has set, just as if it is dissolved when heat has arrived, it is no longer recognized as it was, just so also I, I have been abandoned by all, I have been ruined and out of my house. Sabaeans, you who see, and confusion will come to those whose heart is placed in cities and riches (χρῆμα). But you also have risen against me without pity; so (ὥστε) having seen the wound, fear. What then? Have I asked (αἰτεῖν) you for something? Or do I need your strength so that (ὥστε) you save me from my enemies, or that you tear me from the hand of the powerful? Instruct me, and I will be silent. That in which I have erred, teach it to me, but to what point are the words of the just bad? For I do not ask for strength from you, and your blame will not make me cease to speak, and I will not endure (ἀνέχεσθαι) the sound of your words. Besides that you rise up against an orphan (ὀρφανός), you jump on your comrade. But now I will look you in the face, and I will not lie. Sit down then (οὖν) and do not let violence happen, and

Is not the life of man on the earth a trial, and his life like that of a day laborer? Be it like the servant fearing his master having found the shade, or like the laborer waiting for his wage, thus, I too, have endured hollow months, nights of afflictions have been given to me. When I lie down, I say: When will the morning arrive? And again, when I rise, I say: When will the sun set? And I am filled with pains from evening to morning, my carcass is soiled with bad worms, and my humors make the earth wet when I scrape my sores; my life is faster than a word, and it is lost in a vain hope. Remember

then that my life is a breath and that my eye will not turn back to see happiness; the eye of the one who sees me will no longer look at me; your eyes are upon me, and I am no more; like a cloud disappeared from a clear sky, when man has descended into the abode of the dead, he does not come back up; he will no longer return to his own house, and his dwelling will no longer recognize him. That is why I will not spare my mouth; I will speak, being in anguish (ἀνάγκη) of spirit; I will give issue to the bitterness of my soul with conviction. Am I the sea or a dragon that you should establish a guard over me? I said: My bed will console me, and I will speak with myself on my couch: you frighten me with dreams, and you upset me with visions (ὁράμα). You will release my life (ψυχή) from my spirit (πνεύμα), and my bones

from death; for I will not live forever, to be patient; move away then from me, for my life is empty. What then is man, that you have given him importance or that you pay attention to him, and that you visit him until the morning, and that you limit him for rest? What will be the end of this time when you do not let me go, and when you do not let me swallow my saliva in pain? If I have sinned what will I be able to do to you? You who know the heart of men, why have you put me in a situation to accuse you? Why am I a burden against you? And why have you not forgotten my iniquity and washed my sin? And now I am going to go away into the earth, and at the rising of the morning I will be no more."

the Lord unjust in judging? Will the one who created all things shake justice? If your sons have sinned before him, he has put their iniquities into your hand. As for you, be early before the almighty Lord, to implore him. If you are pure and just, he will hear your prayer, he will give you a dwelling of justice; so that, if your first fortune will have been modest, your last will be immense. Interrogate a primitive generation (γενεά), consult the age (γένος) of our fathers; for we, we are of yesterday and we do not know; it is indeed a shadow that our life is on the earth; is it not they who will instruct you and inform you, and will bring out sentences from your heart? Will the papyrus flower without water! Will the flowering rush grow without absorbing? It is still on its root and it will not be harvested: without absorbing, every plant withers; such will also be the end of every being who forgets

the Lord; for the hope of the impious will be ruined; his house, no one will inhabit it; his tent will be like the spider; if he consolidates his house, it will not hold; if he begins, he will not persist. He is not burned by the sun, and from his bud (?) will come out his branch; he will rest on heaps of stones, and will live in the midst of pebbles (χάλιξ); if the ground absorbs him, it will deny him. Have you not seen likewise that the ruin of the impious will arrive thus, and that something else will come out of the earth. The Lord will not abandon the innocent; but all the gifts of the impious, he will refuse; as for the just, he will fill their mouth with gladness, and their lips with declarations; their enemies will be covered with shame: the dwelling of the impious will be no more."

Job answered, and said: "I know truly that it is so; for how will man be just before the Lord? If he wants to have a right against him, he will not answer him, so as not to contradict (ἀντιλέγειν) a single word of his out of a thousand; for he is wise in his thought; he is powerful and great. Who is the one who has been solid before him and has persisted? He who makes the mountains age without their knowing, who destroys them in his anger; He who shakes the underside of the sky and its foundations ...; He who speaks to the sun, and it does not rise; he seals (the sky) with stars ...; He who stretches out the sky all alone, who walks on the sea as on the earth; He who makes the Pleiades, the evening star (the morning star), Arcturus and the storehouses (ταμιεῖον) of the South; He who makes


[p. 243] {#po.18.p243}

δάδηδηρεθαποο πε ΠΟ ἐπθρεὲεν που πε ΗΕ ἐπε χλλλον ΤΙ ἼῸΣ Ἐἐριμου, εὐϑυτι δλαυδηουόσδει ἡπδηδ ἐροα Σ δα λποετεῖ ΜΡΙΔΈΛΑΙ ὁπ. δ ὁ ταν δαδληλρε πλλ πὲ τππδ- ἼΔΟΘΟ τὲ τῦλλ εὐιδύϑῶςοο δ Ὲ ΟὟ πεῖδκάτα, ἤθους ,ε δεφαπϑ ἄπεαθὸ δον" δὲ ουὐξύαπτ. εἰδὺς- καλῶς ἍῈ ΕΟ Κλλλος ΞΕ ΚΉΟΟ ἘπΟ ΤΕ ΟΗ τ τε. εατε Ἀεὶ Ὑπεούλελλ Ἐρὸν τὰ περ Δ ΚΡη τι δα οδῶκν, εαττ τε ὰ ΔΤῸΚ ΟὝΘΧΗΙ Χαπτεσούλτελι ἢ ἘΡῸΣ πε ᾶτε ἐτταδ ἄλλος. εϑσατε τὰν ἩἸΔΑΛΟΎ ΚΤ ΟὙΌΣ ἐν ἀπεσούσπελλ ἐρὸν Κλ πεν οΎσιο δὲ κε Δαούσπελι
great and unfathomable things, glorious works and wonders without number: If he passes near me, I will not see him; if he passes me by, I will know nothing of it. Likewise, if he strikes, who will react? Or who will say to him: What have you done? It is he himself who turns his face from anger, but the whales (κῆτος) that are under the sky have bowed at a distance from him. If he listens to me, will he judge my words? If I am right, he will not hear me imploring his sentence; if I call and if he listens to me, I will not believe that he has listened to me, for fear that he might make me disappear in a whirlwind (γνόφος). He has multiplied my wounds uselessly; he does not let me breathe: he has filled me with bitterness; when he seizes with violence, who will resist him and his judgment? Even if I were just, my mouth would commit impiety; even if I were blameless,
Ἐρον ααθτταο ἡπεοττ, ἐὸν εν ουσπόφοο. δασ- φτοῦδῶδι ε ἩΔΌΟΜθελ εν οὙπειουτι. ἀχὸ ἄλλον ΔῈ ἐσθ ἀλδϑὶ τᾶν ἀδν, οὐν λλεπ ὃ τΔΡ δάδδλδιϑν εν οὐ σκολα. τῦλλ ἐπι δ Ἷ ἘροΎ τι ΕΘ ΡΔΟ πΈλλ πεαϑάτι. εατε τὰν ὅπΟ ΟὝΘΧΛΗΣ ραν πδεραλεῖδοες Ὅμο. εσατε ΤΡ ΟΝ ΟὙΔΊΔΡΙΚΙ ΔΛ ΔΙ ΕΟ εἰκός, στ τδρ διερλειδοεθηο ζελι δὲ Ῥτνπ ἸΔΌΌΧΗ πλὴν ὅε ΔΌΛΩΙ Κλ Ὁ. ἐασλὲ ᾧδι λκοο κε Οὐ Τ σελ οὐαρι ἀπδιλκαου εν οὐκί. τοοδίλ- πετϑο οεὲ Ῥεν ΟὝλλΟΎ ταὸν ἡηρι δᾶ σεοαδι ππεσλλην ἡ, ΔΎ ΗΟ ΓΔΡ ἘΡΗΙ ἙἸοῖτα τ ΔΟΕΜΟ τπϑὸ Ἀεὲ ἅτε τρε ἴϑαπ ἀδαβλο ἀλλο. ΟὙῸΣ ἄλλον ἤθου πε... "ἢ πδοα Ὁ ὍἈῈ ἀπ δοαοῸ ἐστε οὐπιπόοοεν δοασ οὐορ ΧπουπδΔ. τοῦσε ΟΥΟΣΙ Ἴδιον Χαλλυατα ὑπ ΠΕ ΞΗΟῸ ΤῈ τὰ ΟὙΔΌΑΝ τα ΗΝ τακ πο ΟοὙΌρε πδα. διωδηεξιρι τὰ ὕπδερ- πα ετοδῶει δτιδκύαρως ὅ. ἀπάδο ΕφΗΣ ΟὙῸΒ ιδαιδϑδον, Ὑκιλ πὶ Ῥθετὶ πδλλελοο πηφοῦ ζελλι ΓΔΡ πε χυδαδὶ τὸν ΔΊ ΟΝ τ. ἘΠ δος ΟὙΔΟΕΆπΟ εσλε οὐ Χαττιλλο ὅς. δι ςάκελλ τὰ εν οὐχταν ὃ οΥὐορ, ππδποῦβδον εν άπ εὐουδὰ. δυο τ λα εν οὐσλεν δοοφρει Ἀεὶ ἧκε ἸΔΟΊΟΔΗ.
I shall come out bent. If I have acted as an impious man, I am unaware of it in my soul, but my life is taken away. The great and the strong; the wicked have a death that astonishes, but the just are ridiculed, for they are delivered into the hands of the impious; he covers the faces of the judges and is it not he? My life is swifter than a race: one has fled and has not seen: does a trace exist of the path of ships or of an eagle flying in search of its prey; if I explain myself, I shall forget while speaking, I shall bow my face and I shall sigh; I tremble in all my limbs, for I know that you will not leave me in the condition of an innocent; since I am impious, why am I not dead? If I wash myself in snow, and if I purify myself with pure hands, I am truly dipped in mire, that is why I said: He will destroy in anger.

in his hands that are the souls of all living beings, and the spirits of all men? It is intelligence that scrutinizes words, and the throat that tastes food. (Is) wisdom in a long duration, science, in a long life? With him is found wisdom with strength, to him is the decision with intelligence. If he overturns, who will rebuild? if he shuts people in, who will be able to open? According to whether he holds back the water or lets it advance, he dries up the earth or makes it perish after having overturned it. To him is strength and power, to him is science and intelligence, he who leads captive the arbiters of the world, and who confuses the judges of the earth, he who makes kings sit on thrones, and girds their loins with a belt, who sends priests captive and who overturns the powerful of the earth; who changes the lips of those in whom one had faith and knows the intelligence of the

Who is it who will contest with me, so that I be silent now and desist? You will dispose of me in all; then I shall not hide from your face; remove your hand from upon me, and let your fear not trouble me; then you will call and I shall address the word to you, or else you will speak and I shall answer you. How numerous are my sins and my transgressions? Teach me what they are. Why do you hide from me, and take me for someone who is against you? Move away from him so that he may rest and that he may take pleasure in his life, like the mercenary. The tree has hope; if you cut it, it grows again; and its flower does not fail; for if its root grows old in the earth and its stem dies in the rock, it will grow again at the scent of water, and will give fruits like a new plant. Man, if he dies, goes away; if a man falls, he is no more. It is for a time that the sea fails, that the deserted river dries up: the man who has lain down does not rise again; as long as the sky exists, he will not be restored; they will not be awakened from their sleep. If at least you had kept me in the Amenti, and hidden until your anger passes! if you had fixed a time when you would visit me! we ourselves do not know? What do you understand better than us? is there indeed an old man and an elder among us, more burdened with days than your father? In retribution for your faults, you have been little chastised. You have said very great words: what has your heart meditated? what have your eyes looked at, when you expressed anger in the face of the Lord and you drew such remarks from your mouth? What then is man to be blameless, or the offspring of the woman to be just? If the saints do not have his confidence and if the sky is not pure before him, how much more so the soiled and impure man, drinking iniquity like a beverage (drinker). Also, I am going to inform you, listen to me. What I have seen, I will tell you; the things that the wise will say, their fathers have not hidden them; to them alone has been given the earth, and the stranger has not come to them.

he will enter houses where there will be no one. What those have amassed, others will take, and he will never be rich; his possessions will not be stable: he will not cast a shadow on the earth, and he will not avoid the darkness. His offspring will be withered by the wind, his flower will fall. Let him not believe that he will be durable; for sterility will come to him; his graft will perish before arriving at term, his branch will not bloom; he will be harvested like a green grape before arriving at term; he will fall like an olive flower. The index of the impious is his (the) death; it is the fire that will consume the houses of the stipendiaries; he will conceive pain in his entrails, he will give birth to inanity; his entrails will carry deception."

on the knees with a penetrating instrument; they have run upon me all together. The Lord has delivered me into the hands of the evildoers, he has thrown me among the impious; while I was in peace, he has put me in rout: he has seized the hair of my head, he has uprooted me; he has placed me as a target: they have surrounded me with their lances, sending them into my loins without sparing; they have spilled my gall on the earth; they have put me down, ruin upon ruin; they have run upon me, the powerful; they have thrown a sack on my skin; my power has been extinguished on the earth; my entrails have been burned by tears, there is a shadow on my eyelids. There was nothing unjust in my hands, and my prayer is pure. Earth, do not cover the blood of my flesh and may there be no boundary to my cries. A master force will establish itself in his tent during the night; his ornaments will be filled with sulfur; his roots will dry up below, and his presumed harvest will fall above; his memory will disappear from the earth, and he will have his name erased from his face; he will be cast out from the light into the darkness; he will no longer be known among his people, and his dwelling will no longer be preserved under the sky: but others will live in what belonged to him; the latter have groaned over him; the former had admired him. Such are the dwellings of the violent; such is the place of those who have not known the Lord."

me, and you overwhelm me. Let us admit that I have erred in truth, the error remains with me; if I have said words that should not have been said, my words have gone astray, and I did not say them in their time. Why do you rise up against me and dominate me with your contempt? Know then that it is the Lord who has troubled me and raised his rampart against me. Behold, I will laugh with disdain and I will not speak: I cry out and there is no judgment. I am surrounded on all sides, and I cannot pass. He has put darkness upon my face: he has stripped me of my glory, he has taken my crown from off my head; he has separated me from those who surrounded me, and I have departed; he has cut off my hope like a tree; he has used great anger against me; he has taken me for an enemy; all his trials have come upon me; on my path, I have been surrounded by ambushers; my brothers have turned away from me;

They have known strangers more than me; my companions have been without pity; those who approached me did not look at me, and those who knew my name have forgotten me; the neighbors of my house and my servants, I was a stranger before them; I called my servant, and he did not answer me. I begged with my mouth; I implored my wife; I invoked in supplication the children of my slaves; and they, they have disdained me forever; if I rise, they speak against me; those who know me have abhorred me; those whom I loved have risen against me; my flesh has been putrefied in my skin and my bones are gnawed with their teeth. Have pity on me, have pity on me, O my friends; for the hand of the Lord has touched me. Why do you pursue me like the Lord, and are you not saturated with my flesh? the voice (of my) who reprimands me... have you not learned this from the beginning since man was placed on the earth? The joy of the impious becomes a great failure; the pleasure of the libertines becomes ruin, even if his offerings rise to the sky, and his sacrifice reaches the clouds; indeed, when he believes he is established, then he will be lost definitively; those who know him will say: Where was he? Like a dream that has flown away, one will not find him again; he will fly away like a phantom of the night; the eye has glimpsed him and will not continue; his place will not see him again; inferiors will cause his children to perish; his hands will kindle afflictions; his bones have been satiated with the vices of his youth; they will be laid with him in the earth; if perversity is sweet to his mouth, he will hide it under his tongue; he will not spare it

To fill his belly, he (God) will send upon him the fury of anger, he will send upon him afflictions; he will not be saved from the iron; a bow of bronze will wound him; an arrow will pass through his body; the stars will pass into his habitation; terrors will assail him... an inextinguishable fire will devour him; he who comes upon him will consume his house, the sky will reveal his iniquities, the earth will rise against him; ruin will drag his house until disappearance; a day of anger will come upon him. Such is the portion of the impious man determined by the Lord, with the possession of his goods fixed by Him who watches over everything."

Eliphaz the Temanite answered and said: "Is it not the Lord who teaches intelligence and science? What does it matter to the Lord that you be irreproachable in your actions? and what is (your) advantage, if you display your conduct with him? Will he reprimand you by counting with you and will he come into judgment with you? Is your malice not excessive, your sins innumerable? You have taken the pledge of your brothers uselessly; you have taken the clothes of the stripped; you have not watered the thirsty with water, and you have deprived the hungry of bread; you have been partial to some individuals; and by you, others have become poor on the earth; you have sent widows back into

The clouds hide him, and he will not be seen; he circulates in the perimeter of the sky. Will you keep the secular path that just men have trodden, who were seized prematurely, and whose support is a flowing river, who say: What will the Lord do to us? what will the Almighty bring upon us? goods, the counsel of the impious is far from him. At this sight, the just have laughed; the irreproachable has mocked them: If their fortune had not been destroyed, the fire will devour what remains of them. Be therefore firm, (that we may see) if you are patient, if (then) your fruit will remain in the good. Gather the words of his mouth, and receive his words in your heart. If you convert and humble yourself before the Lord, you remove iniquity from your habitation; you will end up being seated on a rock, like a rock of a torrent of Sophir. The Almighty will be your auxiliary against your enemies; he will purify you like silver passed through the fire; then you will take confidence before the Lord; you will look toward the sky with joy; if you invoke him, he will hear you; he will grant you to accomplish your vows; he will return to you a dwelling of justice, and

no more; what do I know in the last? he has made the left, and I have not grasped; he will hide on the right, and I will not see him; for he already knows my way, and he has tested me like gold; I will walk in his commandments; for I have kept his ways, and I will not turn away from his commandments, and I will not abandon them; I have hidden his words in my bosom. If he himself has judged thus, who is he who would contradict him? for what he has willed, he has done... That is why I have hastened toward him, and warned, I have been afraid of him. (That is why I will hasten before him, I will look and I will be afraid of him.) The Lord has brought weakness upon my heart, the Almighty has rushed against me. I did not think that he would bring darkness upon me; it is the obscurity that has enveloped my face.

Why have the hours been hidden from the Lord? The impious have crossed their borders, taking the herd with its shepherd; they have taken the donkey of the orphans and put the cow of the widow in pledge; they have forced the weak to turn away from the path of justice, and the quiet people of the earth have hidden together; they have been like donkeys in the countryside, having come out of the habits they had... They have harvested a field that was not theirs and before the time; weak people have worked the vineyards of the impious without wages and without food; they have made a naked crowd lie down without clothes; they have taken what covered their person; they are wet by the seepings of the mountains, because they have no shelters, they have covered themselves with the rock. They have torn the orphan from the breast; they

have lowered the fallen. They have made people lie down naked by violence; from the hungry they have taken their bread. They have ambushed by fraud in tightened places, and have not known the way of the just, who have been driven from their cities and their houses, the soul of the little children pushing great groans. But he, why has he not watched over these while they were on the earth? They have not known, they have not known the way of justice and have not walked in his paths. Having known their works, he has delivered them to the darkness, and in the night he will be like a thief; the eye of the adulterer has kept itself for the darkness saying: No eye sees me, and he has believed that they would hide his face. They have dug to enter houses in the obscurity; by day, they have consigned themselves; they have not known

the light; the dawn is the shadow of death that will arrive for them at the same time... He is light on the surface of the water; their portion will be cursed on the earth; his plants will appear dried up on the ground; for they have taken the sheaves of the orphans; then one remembered his sin, and he disappeared like a mist of dew; one will punish him for what he has done; every iniquitous man will be broken like a useless tree; they have not done good to her who is sterile; they will not have pity on him who is without a wife. In anger he has overturned the weak; if he rises again, let him not trust in his life; if he is sick, let him not hope to recover, he will rather fall into sickness. If he gives an order to the moon, it does not shine; the stars are not pure before him: how much more the man who is worth nothing, and the son of man who is a worm?"

impious, and those who stand against me like the loss of the libertines, for what is the hope of the impious so that he may hold? if he trusts in the Lord, will he be saved? will the Lord hear his prayer?... will he find any assurance before him? if he cries toward him, will he listen to him? As for me, I will teach you what is in the hands of the Lord, and I will not deceive about what depends on the Almighty. You know well all that vain things come out of what is vain. This is the share of impious people from the Lord, and the possession of the powerful will come out from upon them from the Almighty; if his sons have been numerous, they will be for the massacre; if they have taken strength, they will beg; (those who survive him will die), and no one will have pity on their widows. for he has imposed an order upon the darkness and he himself searches out all the extremes, the light or the darkness in the shadow of death, the cutting of a torrent in the dust; those who forget the way of justice fail, isolated from men. The earth, from which bread shall come forth, is turned up in its own depths like fire; its stones are the deposit of sapphire, and their gold is one with the earth. The path, the bird does not know it; the eye of the vulture has not seen it: the sons of boasters have not set foot there, the lion has not passed there. He has stretched his hand over the hard cornerstone; he has torn rocks from their foundations; he has divided the violence of the rivers: everything that is glorious, my eye has seen; he has laid bare the depths of the rivers: he has made his power appear in the light.

But wisdom, where has it been found? What is the place of understanding? No mortal knows the way of it, and it shall not be found among men. The abyss has said: It is not in me: and the sea has said: It is not with me... Money shall not be given for its exchange; it shall not be compared with the gold of Ophir in a precious object, nor with sapphire; gold and crystal shall not be assimilated to it; vessels of gold are not its equivalent; it shall not make one think of coral (?) nor of rock crystal (?); let wisdom be carried above treasures (?), the topaz of Ethiopia shall not equal it, it shall not be compared with precious gold. Wisdom, where is it found? What is the path of understanding? All men are ignorant of it, and it is hidden from the birds of the sky. Ruin

I was clothed with judgment as with a cloak; I was the eye of the blind and the foot of the lame; I was the father of the weak; the cause that I did not know, I studied: I broke the teeth of the evildoers, and from between their teeth I made what they had stolen come out. I said: I shall advance in age like a palm trunk; I shall live long; my root has appeared over the waters, and the dew shall be on my harvest. My glory vanishes with me, and my bow shall go in my hand. Hearing me, they paid attention; they were silent at my advice; they added nothing to my words, and rejoiced when I spoke with them; like a thirsty land waits for rain, so they waited for my word. to fraud—but I have been placed in a just balance and the Lord knows my innocence—if my foot has turned from the path, if my heart has followed my eye, if I have touched with my hands corrupting gifts: let me sow, and let others consume; let me be without roots on the earth. If my heart has bent toward the wife of another...; let my young children be slapped! For the fury of anger cannot be contained, if there is violation of another's wife; it is a fire burning everywhere; the place where it penetrates, it destroys radically. If I have bypassed the right of one of my servants or maids having a lawsuit against me, what shall I do then if the Lord questions me? How shall I answer him, if he visits me? Is it not in the same manner that I was formed in the womb, that they were also? We were born

if I have stretched my hand over incalculable sums; until we see the luminous sun cease its light or the moon fail: they have no will. If my heart has been deceived in secret or by stealth; if I have put my hand to my mouth kissing it; that too shall be counted to me for a great fault; for I have denied the Most High Lord to his face. If I have rejoiced at the fall of my enemy, and my heart has said: It is well; let my ear hear my curse; let me be turned into derision and offended by my people. of Ausitis, Elihu was very irritated against Job, because he had said before the Lord: I am just; he was also very irritated against his three companions, because they could not answer Job, and regarded him as an impious man. Elihu had waited to answer Job... And Elihu, son of Barachel the Buzite, took the floor and said: "I am inferior by my age; you, you are old men. For that, I have been silent, fearing to show you my knowledge. I said: The time is not for me to speak. But it is not those who have many years who know wisdom; it is a spirit that is in men, the breath of the Almighty that instructs us. It is not those of a great age who are the wise, nor the old men who are judicious. That is why I say: Listen to me, and I will teach you what I know. Well then, Job, listen to my words... for I have opened my mouth and my tongue has spoken. My heart is clean in my words; the judgment of my lips will recall pure things: it is a holy spirit that has formed me, it is the breath of the Almighty that informs me. If you can, answer me to these things: but wait, stop for me, I will do it for you. You have been formed of mud as we have been ourselves. The fear of me will not trouble you, and my hand will not weigh heavily upon you; but you have said to my ears; I have heard the sound of your words: yes, you have said: I am just; I have not sinned; I am irreprehensible; I have not defaulted; but he has found a grievance against me: he has counted me as an enemy; he has put my foot in the brush; he has watched all my steps. If a thousand angels of death surround him, not one of them will kill him if he has projected in his heart to return to the Lord, to confess to man his guilt and to reveal his offense. that no visit is made to man: for there is a visit to him on the part of the Lord. That is why, you, wise of heart, listen to me: May it not happen to me to be impious before the Lord, nor to trouble the truth before the Almighty: he has recompensed man according to what he has done; he will find man in his paths. Do you think that the Lord will do an injustice, or that the Almighty, who has created the earth, will disturb it? Who is he who has created the regions under the sky and everything that is found there? If he wanted to contain himself and retain the spirit near him, all of a sudden all flesh would die, every man would return to the earth, from where he was fashioned. If you are not instructed, listen to these things; lend an ear to the sound of my words. If you are the one who hates prevaricators, you will destroy the artisans of evil, being

eternally just; he is impious who says to a king: You have transgressed; he is impious toward leaders, he who does not respect someone in dignity, and does not know how to render honor to the great, nor admire their face; but vain will be the profit of those who have acclaimed and implored a man; for they have transgressed; they have defrauded. But he, he is an overseer of the actions of men, nothing is hidden from him of what they do: there is no place where those who commit transgression can hide... For the Lord sees everything; he penetrates unfathomable things, glorious things, and things impossible to count; he knows their works, will overturn their nights, and will humble them. He extinguishes the impious: they appear before him, because they have turned away from the law of God, and have not known his sentences,

If I have practiced iniquity, I will continue no more. Will you render of yourself, because you have rejected? because it is you who have chosen and not I; what do you know that he will say? That is why the wise of heart will say these things, a wise man has heard my word. But Job has not spoken with discretion; his words are not sensible. And now there is no longer anyone who experiences his anger: and no one knows gravely the defection. Job has opened his mouth in vain; he has rendered his words grave through ignorance."

The Lord resumed again, and said to Job from the interior of a cloud: "No! but gird your loins, like a strong man; I will question you; you, answer me. Do you reject my judgment? do you think that I will have pity on you, otherwise than for you to appear just? Will your arm be able to fight against the Lord? and with your voice will you thunder like him? Take for yourself elevation and power; clothe yourself with glory and honor; send angels in your anger; humiliate all the insulters; extinguish the proud; make the impious disappear suddenly, let them rot together in the earth; fill their faces with shame; then I will declare that your right hand can save. But behold an animal, Behemoth, beside you, which eats grass like the oxen;

its strength is in its loins; its power in the navel of its belly; it has stiffened its tail like a cypress, its muscles are intertwined; its ribs are ribs of bronze, and its back is cast iron. It is the first of the creatures of the Lord, which he has made so that his angels may amuse themselves with it. It has departed on a high mountain, it has made the joy of the quadrupeds of Tartarus; it has rested under trees of every kind, under the papyrus, the bamboo and the flowering rush; over it cast their shadow great trees with branches and shoots of the ground. If the great waters arrive, it does not feel it; it is convinced that the Jordan will rush into its mouth. Who will take it by its eye, and gluing it, will pierce its nostrils? ... with a ring? Will it speak to you with fear, supplication and submission? will it make a pact with you? will it be your servant forever? will you play with it as with a bird? will you attach it as a sparrow is by a child? Will peoples feed on it and will the nations of Phoenicia share it? If all the boats gathered, they could not carry a skin of its tail nor its head in fishermen's boats. But you will put your hand on it, remembering the power of struggle that is in your body, and that it may not return again. Have you not seen it; have you not been astonished by what I say? Do you not fear that it is prepared for me? for who fights against me? who stands against me? who resists, if everything that is under the sky is mine? I will not be silent about it, a trait of its

ΟΕΘΟΎΜῚ ΟΥὙΟΣ ἀποσφρααπ, δρεὲ οὐσατεν τατες λον εν πεαδῖσδο. πε ἈῈ σεοὸν ΧΑΦΡΗΤ ἀτποοσ ἅτε ΘΑπδτοοΎν. δόον λον θεν ρας τε ΘΑ δλλττλο χρύλλλ ΕΛΛΟΣΣ οὐὔοὸρ δύλορ, εὸν ἧξε δάπελο ἡχρίλλα ιν
power is that it has pity on its equal. Who can discover the surface of its garment? who can penetrate into the bonds of its breastplate? who will be able to open the doors of its face? Terror surrounds its teeth; its intestines are snakes of bronze; its shell like an emery stone; it is attached from one point to the other; the air cannot penetrate it. Each row holds to its neighbor; they are together and will not escape; a light is produced by its sneezing; its eyes are like the morning star; from its mouth come out burning lamps of fire, burning coals gush ...; its breath is made of sparks ...: strength resides on its neck. Destruction runs before it. The flesh of its body holds together; everything will flow over it, it will not move. Its heart is hardened like stone; it is rigid like an anvil that one cannot move. If it turns, the

terror seizes the four-footed animals that leap upon the earth; if spears fall upon him, they will do nothing to him, it is a javelin thrown at a breastplate. Iron is to him like straw; bronze like wood without strength; a bronze bow will not wound him; thrown stones count for him as hay; hammers, as reeds; he mocks a shaking with flames of fire. His bed is made of sharp stones; all the gold of the sea is under him like a stone that does not count; he makes the ocean boil like a cauldron; the sea is to him like a perfume vase, (the tartarus of the abyss like a captive),

the abyss like a promenade. There is nothing on earth that resembles him, being made for the angels to amuse themselves with, he looks at the heights, and he is king of all that is in the waters."

Job answered and said to the Lord: "I know that you are powerful in all things, and that nothing is impossible for you. Who can hide a plan from you? sparing words, does he think to escape you? Who will teach me what I do not know? great and wonderful things that I was ignorant of? Listen to me, Lord, that I may also speak. I will question you, and you, instruct me. At first I heard you by voice and ears; now my eye sees you. That is why I despise myself, I am confounded; I regard myself as dust and ashes."

It happened, after Job had spoken thus, that the Lord said to Eliphaz the Temanite: "You have sinned, you and your two companions; for you have not spoken true words before me as my servant Job. And now procure for yourselves seven calves and seven rams, and go to my servant Job; he will make a sacrifice for you... If I did not show partiality to him, if it were not for him, I would have destroyed you; for you have not spoken justly before my servant Job." Eliphaz the Temanite went away with Bildad the Shuhite and Zophar the Naamathite; and they did as the Lord had told them; their sins were forgiven because of Job. The Lord had raised up Job; he prayed for his three companions. God forgave their sins. The Lord doubled all the goods of Job that he had formerly; he gave them to him in double.


[p. 336] {#po.18.p336}

All his brothers and his sisters heard of everything that had happened to him; they came to him with all those who had known him formerly. Eating and drinking with him, they congratulated him and admired everything that the Lord had brought him. They each gave him a lamb and four drachmas of precious gold. The Lord blessed the end of Job more than his beginnings. His livestock were: fourteen thousand sheep, six thousand camels, a thousand pairs of oxen, a thousand donkeys. There were born to him seven sons and three daughters: he called the first: Day; the second: Cassia; the third: Horn of Amalthea; and among all those who are under heaven, none were found as beautiful as the daughters of Job. Their father gave them an inheritance among their brothers.

Job lived after the calamity another one hundred and nine years; his years that he lived were two hundred and forty. Job saw his sons and the sons of his sons up to the fourth generation; and Job died old and full of years. It is written further concerning him that he will rise again with those whom the Lord will raise up.


[p. 338] {#po.18.p338}

This has been interpreted from the book in Syriac. He lived in the land of Ausitis, on the borders of Idumea and Arabia. His name was at first Jobab. He took an Arab wife; she bore him a son whose name was Ennon. His own father was Zerah, one of the sons of Esau; his mother, Bozrah; so that he was the fifth after Abraham. Here are the kings who reigned in Edom, the country which he himself governed:
ΔΆρδδν. ΟΥὙῸΒ ΔΙ ΠΕ ΤΠΟΎῬΟΥ ΔῈ ΕἸΔΎΕΡΟΥῬΟ Ῥεν τῶν ἴχαφά εἰδαερρχν πὰ δα τϑοῦσι πὲ δλδκ τον ἡδέα οὐορ ὥρδε ἡπεα- βᾶκε πε πεπδβᾶ. επεπολ Ἀδλάκ ΔΒ πετεΎ- ΟΎΤ ἔρος κε παλὶ λεπετοδ ᾧδν Δοολὶ ττΓΈλλίανι τεὸν εν Ἴοτελλάνστηο ἡχρα. πλεπετοά ᾧ δι ΔῈ Δδῖν πῶπρι ἡδρδ Φη εἰδοκύλαςν Καλλδντδλλ Ἐπ πκον ἄλλαι οὐορ Ὅρδν ἡπεαβάκι πὲ κεύθει. περ Ὧε ΕἸΔῸΣ σιάρου εθοδῷ οὐῶπρι ἅπε ΟΥΑΙ ἈΕῚ ΠΗΟΔῸ ΠπΟΎΌΡΟ ἥτε πτσελλάπίσαν πελλ ΑΔ δῶν.
The first is Balac, son of Beor; the name of his city Dinhabah; after Balac, Jobab who is called Job; after him, Husham, a leader who came out of the land of the Temanites; after him, Hadad, son of Bedad, who cut Midian to pieces in the plain of Moab; the name of his city was Avith. His companions who came to him were Eliphaz, son of one of the sons of Esau, king of Teman; then Bildad, tyrant of the Shuhites; then Zophar, king of the Naamathites,


[p. 339] {#po.18.p339}
πντορδπποο ἧτε τοῦδ εν πελλ ΑΔ Ρ ΠπΟΎΡΟ ὑττε ΤΠΛΑΤΝΕΟΟ, Με ιετιεροσια πεν τας οὐονῦ τὰ πενπεφουτα ἅτε τῶ, εοδῷ το. Ἀλλ τὶ, οὐαῷὰρ Ἶ. εουο ὁ ἔ. πετπερουίαν ἧττε ΟῚ ταολϑεν πελὶ τὰ ἃν ἯΔΙ ΠΡΟ.
The discourses in Job. There are twelve discourses of Job; of Eliphaz three; of Bildad, three; of Zophar, two; of Elihu, five. The discourses of God speaking to Job, four. That is all.


[p. 340] {#po.18.p340}
